# Joint family and its businesses — the Randhawa family (seven books)

**About this book:** Joint family: one joint fund, three sub-family books, three family businesses. Three brothers — Amarjeet, Lakhbeer and Lakhwinder Randhawa — run a joint household. The common money lives in the **Randhawa Family Fund** (the joint fund); each brother's household has its own sub-family book; the family owns **Randhawa Agriculture** and **Randhawa Real Estate** outright and shares **Randhawa Brick Field** 3 : 2 with an outside partner, Nirmaljeet Gill, who is not on the app. Each book stands on its own: every movement between two books is entered in both, as a matching pair, and the Family Reconciliation table at the end shows each pair agreeing. Assumptions stated, not given: Indu is Amarjeet's wife; Aman is Lakhbeer's son and runs the farm day to day.

**Period:** 01 Apr 2025 – 31 Mar 2026 (FY 2025-26) · every month closed · year closed. Sample report — the figures are illustrative.

| Person | Role in this example |
|:---|:---|
| Amarjeet Randhawa | eldest brother · admin of the fund, Agriculture and Brick Field · head of his sub-family book |
| Lakhbeer Randhawa | brother · admin of the fund and Brick Field · head of his sub-family book · approves members' over-limit entries in the fund |
| Lakhwinder Randhawa | brother · admin of the fund and Real Estate · head role in Agriculture · head of his sub-family book |
| Indu Randhawa | Amarjeet's wife · member of the fund (limit ₹5,000) and of Amarjeet's Family (limit ₹3,000) |
| Aman Randhawa | Lakhbeer's son · member of the fund (₹5,000), of Lakhbeer's Family (₹3,000) and of Agriculture (₹20,000) — holds the monthly farm-labour advance |
| Harjeet Randhawa | Lakhwinder's wife · member of the fund (₹5,000), of Lakhwinder's Family (₹5,000) and of Real Estate (₹5,000) |
| Supreet Randhawa | Lakhwinder's daughter · member of Lakhwinder's Family (limit ₹2,000) |
| Nirmaljeet Gill | outside partner in the Brick Field (weight 2 of 5) — **not on the app**; his takeouts are entered by Lakhbeer on his instruction |

How to read it: amounts are in ₹, with paise shown only when they are not zero. Every statement is written the way a ledger book is, with the plain meaning first and the true side in brackets — Money in (Dr), Money out (Cr), You will get (Dr), You will give (Cr). An account statement runs Date · Entry · Particulars (*To …* on the Dr side, *By …* on the Cr side) · Dr · Cr · Balance · Side; it opens with the balance brought forward and ends with the closing balance carried down, the totals and the opening balance for the new year. Income and expense heads have no opening balance and end with their total for the year.

## Book: Randhawa Family Fund — Joint Fund Book

Joint fund book · started 01 Apr 2025 · members: Amarjeet Randhawa — admin (karta), no limit; Lakhbeer Randhawa — admin, no limit; Lakhwinder Randhawa — admin, no limit; Indu Randhawa — member, entry limit ₹5,000; Aman Randhawa — member, entry limit ₹5,000; Harjeet Randhawa — member, entry limit ₹5,000.

Members' entries over their limit are approved by Lakhbeer Randhawa; Amarjeet's and Lakhbeer's own entries would go to Lakhwinder Randhawa (none arose — admins have no limit).

### 4. Corrections and approvals

- **Rejected:** RF-030 (14 Jul 2025, ₹7,500, entered by Indu Randhawa over the ₹5,000 limit) was rejected by Lakhbeer Randhawa on 16 Jul 2025 — reason: *paid from Amarjeet's household cash, not from the fund — belongs in Amarjeet's Family book*. The app entered the reversal RF-031 on its own; both entries stay visible and together they come to nil.
- **Approved after review:** RF-046 (12 Sep 2025, ₹6,200) by Aman Randhawa was over the member's limit of ₹5,000. It was entered at once and marked for review, and Lakhbeer Randhawa approved it on 13 Sep 2025. The approval changed no figure.

### 8. What we have and what we owe (position) as on 31 Mar 2026

What we have and what we owe on 31 Mar 2026: money in hand and in the bank, what others will pay, advances held (Dr), against what is still to be paid — people and shops, bank loans and cards, partners' balances (Cr). Each account sits on the side its balance falls. The difference is the **Family's own funds (accumulated surplus)**, a figure worked out from the others and not an account of its own.

### 5. Months closed and cash counts

What the business has and what it owes on 31 Mar 2026: money in hand and in the bank, what others will pay, advances held (Dr), against what is still to be paid — people and shops, bank loans and cards, partners' balances (Cr). Each account sits on the side its balance falls. The difference is the **Owner's funds in the business (accumulated surplus)**, a figure worked out from the others and not an account of its own.

## Book: Randhawa Real Estate — Family Business Book

Business book (just me) · started 01 Apr 2025 · members: Lakhwinder Randhawa — admin, no limit; Harjeet Randhawa — member, entry limit ₹5,000.

Ownership: **Just me** (family-owned). Each tenant has one account: a security deposit held is a **credit** on it (you will give it back), the month's rent is entered as a debit on the 1st, and each receipt credits it. ### 4. Corrections and approvals

No correction, reversal or approval was needed in this book this year.

### 5. Months closed and cash counts

Cash is counted at the close of the month's last day and any difference is entered that day; the month is then closed a few days later. A month is closed only when nothing is waiting: no unexplained money, no entry waiting for approval, no advance request waiting.

### 6. Tenants — deposits, rent owed and a tenant leaving

Each tenant has one account. The security deposit is a **credit** (you will give it back); the month's rent is a **debit** on the 1st; each receipt credits it. **How the age of arrears is counted:** each receipt clears the oldest unpaid rent first, and a deposit held counts against the next rent due — so a tenant's arrears show as a Dr balance only once they are more than the deposit held. Dr Kaur's Clinic could be three months behind and still show a Cr balance; Sharma Family hold no deposit, so their arrears show plainly.

| Person / shop | Balance | You will get ₹ | Owed since | Days | How old |
|:---|---:|---:|:---|---:|:---|
| Dr Kaur's Clinic (tenant) A/c | 36,000 Cr | — | — | — | nothing to get |
| Sharma Family (tenant) A/c | 18,000 Dr | 18,000 | 01 Feb 2026 | 58 | more than 30 days (amber) |
| Mehta Mobile Shop (tenant) A/c | 0 — | — | — | — | nothing to get |
| Sidhu Pharmacy (tenant) A/c | 30,000 Cr | — | — | — | nothing to get |

Mehta Mobile Shop left on 31 Jan 2026: January's rent (RR-092) was entered as due, no money was received, and the ₹24,000 deposit was adjusted against it; the remaining ₹12,000 was refunded on 02 Feb 2026 (RR-099), closing the account at nil. Dr Kaur's Clinic's ₹36,000 deposit and Sidhu Pharmacy's ₹30,000 deposit carry into the new year as Cr balances (see the year end).

## Book: Randhawa Brick Field — Shared Business Book

Business book (shared with others) · started 01 Apr 2025 · members: Lakhbeer Randhawa — admin, no limit; Amarjeet Randhawa — admin, no limit.

Ownership: **Shared with others** — Randhawa family (share 3) and Nirmaljeet Gill (share 2). The kiln and its land are not on the books (a thing owned is not yet an account — a known limit); the partners' opening balances are the working money each put in when the book was created, and Opening Balance / Capital holds the ₹2,000 difference between the ₹4,52,000 net opening position on the books and the ₹4,50,000 put in.

Partners' shares: Randhawa family 3 · Nirmaljeet Gill 2, agreed when the book was created. Decisions that change the partnership need every owner's approval; Nirmaljeet Gill is not on the app, so no such decision — sharing out the profit, interest on capital, a change of shares — could be completed this year. The surplus stays in the business and is carried forward (see the position and the year end).

### 6. Partners — what each put in, took out and is owed

| Partner | Share | Put in (opening + own pocket) ₹ | Took out ₹ | Interest ₹ | Profit share ₹ | Balance | Meaning |
|:---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|:---|
| Randhawa Family — Partner Current A/c | 3 | 2,70,000 | — | — | — | **2,70,000 Cr** | the business owes them |
| Nirmaljeet Gill — Partner Current A/c | 2 | 2,05,000 | 3,00,000 | — | — | **95,000 Dr** | they owe the business |

#### Could the business pay the partners out today?

**The business could pay every partner out today** — cash and bank ₹4,62,620, partners' credit balances ₹2,70,000.

A note the app shows (when a partner is more than ₹1,00,000 above the others' average): *"Randhawa Family has ₹1,82,500 more with the business than the group average"* — information, never a demand.

- Pay a partner out from the business bank account — offered; needs the cash.
- Settle between partners outside the business — offered; not taken.
- **Carry the balances forward (chosen):** every partner's balance closes at the year end and re-opens as a dated opening balance for the new year — never a remembered number. See the year-end section.

No profit was shared out and no interest on capital was credited this year: both need every owner's approval, and Nirmaljeet Gill is not on the app, so his approval could not be recorded. The year's surplus is therefore carried forward in the *Partners' funds in the business* line (see the position and the year end). The Randhawa family took nothing out this year; Gill's takeouts are ordinary partner entries.

## Family Reconciliation — every pair of books agrees, as on 31 Mar 2026

Every movement between two books is entered in both. For every pair of books, what one says it is owed is exactly what the other says it owes, so the two balances add up to nil. The side is shown as the ledger shows it, with the plain words in brackets.

| Pair | Fund's book says | Other book says | Entries in the pair | Together |
|:---|:---|:---|---:|:---:|
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Amarjeet's Family | Amarjeet's Family (sub-family book): 2,11,700 Dr (that book owes the fund) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 2,11,700 Cr (this book owes the fund) | 13 | **0** ✓ |
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Lakhbeer's Family | Lakhbeer's Family (sub-family book): 2,16,000 Dr (that book owes the fund) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 2,16,000 Cr (this book owes the fund) | 12 | **0** ✓ |
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Lakhwinder's Family | Lakhwinder's Family (sub-family book): 2,64,000 Dr (that book owes the fund) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 2,64,000 Cr (this book owes the fund) | 12 | **0** ✓ |
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Randhawa Agriculture | Randhawa Agriculture (family business book): 9,50,000 Cr (the fund owes that book) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 9,50,000 Dr (the fund owes this book) | 4 | **0** ✓ |
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Randhawa Real Estate | Randhawa Real Estate (family business book): 2,40,000 Cr (the fund owes that book) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 2,40,000 Dr (the fund owes this book) | 4 | **0** ✓ |
| Randhawa Family Fund ↔ Randhawa Brick Field | Randhawa Brick Field (shared business book): 90,000 Dr (that book owes the fund) | Randhawa Family Fund (joint family book): 90,000 Cr (this book owes the fund) | 2 | **0** ✓ |

Reading these balances: a Dr balance in the fund means that book owes the fund (the allowances the fund paid out; the Brick Field loan); a Cr balance in the fund means the fund owes that book (the surplus the businesses sent). Business surplus reaches the fund through these pairs, not as income of the fund, so the fund's own income and expenses show only its common spending.
